Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to do things, because I do get through them. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it has changed some.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasurable at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “not pleasurable at all,” exactly… I mean, I can still get through things, and sometimes there are small moments that are nice. It’s just more muted than it used to be, I guess. Like I’m there, but I’m not really feeling it the way I think I should. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find pleasurable but now find you have no interest in at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if there’s anything I have no interest in at all. I guess I still try to do things, or I tell myself I should… it’s just harder to care about them like I used to. But I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more that I’m less interested, not completely uninterested, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe a little, yeah. I don’t want to say I’ve completely lost interest, because I still do some things and I don’t hate them or anything. It’s more like I don’t look forward to them the way I used to. But I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say I’m unable to. That sounds really absolute, and I don’t think it’s like I can’t feel anything good. I can sometimes feel okay about things, or have little moments that are nice. It just maybe doesn’t hit the same way as it used to. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I guess I still wouldn’t say I’m totally unable to feel pleasure. Like… that sounds stronger than what I know how to say. Sometimes things are still kind of nice, or I can tell they should be nice, if that makes sense. It’s just not always as enjoyable as I think it should be. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are things that used to bring you pleasure but now feel empty or unfulfilling?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Maybe some things feel less… satisfying than they used to, but I don’t know if I’d call them empty exactly. That sounds kind of dramatic, and I don’t want to overstate it. I guess I notice I’ll do something that should feel good, and it’s more like, “okay, that was fine,” instead of really feeling much from it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble feeling happy or getting enjoyment out of things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say most of the time. Maybe more often than I want to admit, but I’m not sure how to measure that. I can still enjoy things a little sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good. It’s just… a lot of things feel kind of muted, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to do things, because I do get through them. But small stuff feels like it takes way more out of me than it should—like filling out inspection logs at work, or driving to basketball, or even washing my hair. It’s like I start already kind of drained.\n\nAnd even on my days off, resting doesn’t really make me feel refilled. I don’t know if that counts as less energy, but it feels that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just been feeling really low for a long time. Like, the last couple years, there’s this kind of heavy, empty feeling that’s there most of the time. Even when I’m at work, or at church, or with people I care about, it doesn’t really go away. I’ve tried to just push through it, and maybe it’s not as bad as it could be, but… it’s been hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy things that you used to like, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that the right way. I mean… I still do some of the things I used to like, and sometimes they’re okay, I guess. But it’s not really the same as before. It’s harder to feel excited about them, if that makes sense. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it has changed some.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasurable at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “not pleasurable at all,” exactly… I mean, I can still get through things, and sometimes there are small moments that are nice. It’s just more muted than it used to be, I guess. Like I’m there, but I’m not really feeling it the way I think I should. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find pleasurable but now find you have no interest in at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if there’s anything I have no interest in at all. I guess I still try to do things, or I tell myself I should… it’s just harder to care about them like I used to. But I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more that I’m less interested, not completely uninterested, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe a little, yeah. I don’t want to say I’ve completely lost interest, because I still do some things and I don’t hate them or anything. It’s more like I don’t look forward to them the way I used to. But I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say I’m unable to. That sounds really absolute, and I don’t think it’s like I can’t feel anything good. I can sometimes feel okay about things, or have little moments that are nice. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I guess I still wouldn’t say I’m totally unable to feel pleasure. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just been feeling really low for a long time. Like, the last couple years, there’s this kind of heavy, empty feeling that’s there most of the time. Even when I’m at work, or at church, or with people I care about, it doesn’t really go away. I’ve tried to just push through it, and maybe it’s not as bad as it could be, but… it’s been hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy things that you used to like, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that the right way. I mean… I still do some of the things I used to like, and sometimes they’re okay, I guess. But it’s not really the same as before. It’s harder to feel excited about them, if that makes sense. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… yeah, it has changed some.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy that you no longer find pleasurable at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “not pleasurable at all,” exactly… I mean, I can still get through things, and sometimes there are small moments that are nice. It’s just more muted than it used to be, I guess. Like I’m there, but I’m not really feeling it the way I think I should. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to find pleasurable but now find you have no interest in at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure if there’s anything I have no interest in at all. I guess I still try to do things, or I tell myself I should… it’s just harder to care about them like I used to. But I don’t want to overstate it. Maybe it’s more that I’m less interested, not completely uninterested, if that makes sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe a little, yeah. I don’t want to say I’ve completely lost interest, because I still do some things and I don’t hate them or anything. It’s more like I don’t look forward to them the way I used to. But I’m not sure if that counts as losing interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d say I’m unable to. That sounds really absolute, and I don’t think it’s like I can’t feel anything good. I can sometimes feel okay about things, or have little moments that are nice. It just maybe doesn’t hit the same way as it used to. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I guess I still wouldn’t say I’m totally unable to feel pleasure. Like… that sounds stronger than what I know how to say. Sometimes things are still kind of nice, or I can tell they should be nice, if that makes sense. It’s just not always as enjoyable as I think it should be. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are things that used to bring you pleasure but now feel empty or unfulfilling?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Maybe some things feel less… satisfying than they used to, but I don’t know if I’d call them empty exactly. That sounds kind of dramatic, and I don’t want to overstate it. I guess I notice I’ll do something that should feel good, and it’s more like, “okay, that was fine,” instead of really feeling much from it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble feeling happy or getting enjoyment out of things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say most of the time. Maybe more often than I want to admit, but I’m not sure how to measure that. I can still enjoy things a little sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good. It’s just… a lot of things feel kind of muted, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to do things, because I do get through them. But small stuff feels like it takes way more out of me than it should—like filling out inspection logs at work, or driving to basketball, or even washing my hair. It’s like I start already kind of drained.\n\nAnd even on my days off, resting doesn’t really make me feel refilled. I don’t know if that counts as less energy, but it feels that way.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low mood, can you estimate the exact timeframe?\n\nPatient: I think… about two years and four months, if I’m counting right. It kind of started around when I had to stop taking community college classes because I couldn’t really make the money and my work schedule fit anymore.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the exact start, but that’s the point I keep coming back to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a little. I don’t think it’s been anything extreme, but it does go up and down. Some days I’ll go most of my shift without eating because nothing sounds good, and then later I’ll just grab something from the vending machine because I’m tired and it’s easy.\n\nI’ve noticed my work pants fitting differently from month to month, so… I guess that probably counts as a change?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had okay moments, like if I’m at church or with people I care about, or I get through a decent day at work. But it doesn’t feel like I’ve had a stretch where that heaviness actually lifted.\n\nFor the last couple years it’s more like there’s this low, empty feeling kind of sitting there most of the time. I can still act normal, I think, but inside it doesn’t really go away. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you felt consistently depressed and unable to experience pleasure most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but for the last couple years I’ve felt low most of the time. Even when I’m at work or at church or around people I care about, there’s still this heavy, empty feeling there, like it doesn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there have definitely been more than two weeks like that. Does that answer it okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: I guess I still wouldn’t say I’m totally unable to feel pleasure. Like… that sounds stronger than what I know how to say. Sometimes things are still kind of nice, or I can tell they should be nice, if that makes sense. It’s just not always as enjoyable as I think it should be. Is that an okay way to answer it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are things that used to bring you pleasure but now feel empty or unfulfilling?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Maybe some things feel less… satisfying than they used to, but I don’t know if I’d call them empty exactly. That sounds kind of dramatic, and I don’t want to overstate it. I guess I notice I’ll do something that should feel good, and it’s more like, “okay, that was fine,” instead of really feeling much from it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having trouble feeling happy or getting enjoyment out of things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say most of the time. Maybe more often than I want to admit, but I’m not sure how to measure that. I can still enjoy things a little sometimes, so I don’t want to make it sound like nothing ever feels good. It’s just… a lot of things feel kind of muted, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to do things, because I do get through them. But small stuff feels like it takes way more out of me than it should—like filling out inspection logs at work, or driving to basketball, or even washing my hair. It’s like I start already kind of drained.\n\nAnd even on my days off, resting doesn’t really make me feel refilled. I don’t know if that counts as less energy, but it feels that way.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low mood, can you estimate the exact timeframe?\n\nPatient: I think… about two years and four months, if I’m counting right. It kind of started around when I had to stop taking community college classes because I couldn’t really make the money and my work schedule fit anymore.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the exact start, but that’s the point I keep coming back to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah, a little. I don’t think it’s been anything extreme, but it does go up and down. Some days I’ll go most of my shift without eating because nothing sounds good, and then later I’ll just grab something from the vending machine because I’m tired and it’s easy.\n\nI’ve noticed my work pants fitting differently from month to month, so… I guess that probably counts as a change?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods in the past two years where you felt significantly better than you do now?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had okay moments, like if I’m at church or with people I care about, or I get through a decent day at work. But it doesn’t feel like I’ve had a stretch where that heaviness actually lifted.\n\nFor the last couple years it’s more like there’s this low, empty feeling kind of sitting there most of the time. I can still act normal, I think, but inside it doesn’t really go away. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two weeks where you felt consistently depressed and unable to experience pleasure most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but for the last couple years I’ve felt low most of the time. Even when I’m at work or at church or around people I care about, there’s still this heavy, empty feeling there, like it doesn’t really lift.\n\nSo… yes, I guess there have definitely been more than two weeks like that. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from things that normally would bring you happiness most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I wouldn’t say I can’t feel happiness at all… like, sometimes something will still be nice, or I’ll know I should be enjoying it.\n\nBut a lot of the time it feels kind of muted, I guess. Like I’m there, and I’m trying to be present, but it doesn’t hit the same way it used to. I don’t know if that counts as “unable,” though. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this low, empty feeling for most of every day over the last two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think most days, honestly. I still get through work and I still go to church and try to be normal around people, so it’s not like I’m falling apart every second, but that low, empty feeling is kind of there underneath almost all the time.\n\nI guess over the last couple years it hasn’t really lifted for any real length of time. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say almost all activities… that sounds really strong, and I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nIt’s more like things don’t feel as enjoyable as they should. I can still do them, and sometimes I can tell something is supposed to be good, but the feeling is kind of dulled. So… maybe some loss of pleasure, but I’m not sure about “almost all,” if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one period of feeling this low and empty in your life?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate it into “periods,” if that makes sense. I mean… I’ve had times before where I felt down, but I don’t know that I’ve kept track of it clearly enough to say yes, definitely more than one.\n\nIt feels more like something that’s been there in the background, not always in a neat start-and-stop way. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in your life before the last two years where you felt similarly low and empty?\n\nPatient: Before the last two years… I’m not sure I can point to a clear time like that. I mean, I’ve had normal ups and downs, but the thing that really brought me here is that for the last couple years it’s felt pretty constant.\n\nEven when I’m at work, or church, or with people I care about, there’s this heavy, empty feeling underneath that doesn’t really go away. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this low and empty before the last two years, and then gotten better for a while before feeling it again?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it was like that before, where it clearly happened and then got better and came back.\n\nIt’s more that this stretch has been going on for about… two years and four months, I guess.
